## Building on Strengths Linde Bangladesh Limited Annual Report 2022

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Our Vision

We are committed to fulfilling our vision to be the best performing global industrial gases and engineering company, where our people deliver innovative and sustainable solutions for our customers in a connected world.

<!-- image -->

## Our Mission

We live our mission of making our world more productive every day. Through our high-quality solutions, technologies and services we are making our customers more successful and helping to sustain and protect our planet.

<!-- image -->

## Our Strategic Direction

We will work as One Linde to build on our individual and collective strengths across a larger global footprint. We will increase network density in core industrial gas geographies and will execute our robust backlog. We are fully committed to capturing the full value of the merger.

## Our Core Values

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

We put safety first. We believe all incidents are preventable, and our goal is no harm to people, communities or the environment. We continuously work to improve our safety culture and performance worldwide.

We always strive to achieve our goals ethically, and with the highest integrity. We expect transparent and respectful interactions between management, employees and our business partners, consistent with our Code of Business Integrity.

We are committed to improving the communities where we live and work. Our charitable contributions, along with employee volunteerism, support initiatives that make important and sustainable contributions to our world.

We embrace diversity and inclusion in order to attract, develop and retain the best talent and build high-performing teams. By hearing all voices and benefiting from diverse opinions, thoughts and perspectives, we achieve our full promise and potential.

<!-- image -->

We hold ourselves accountable for our performance, individually and collectively. We focus both on what we accomplish and how we accomplish it, and we are committed to delivering on individual and company goals.

## Building on Strengths

Building on strengths require a holistic approach.

The 50+ year journey of Linde Bangladesh, which started before the Liberation War, is a story of resilience, progress, and a constant adaption to the changing socio-economic-technological landscape of the nation. Despite all the changes and challenges, we have been able to maintain a strong financial performance and consistently expand our business. This success is anchored in Linde's focus on building a value-based culture of innovation, quality and excellence, investing in development of our workforce and delivering superior customer experience. Linde Bangladesh Limited has been and would continue to be a silent partner of progress for the nation.

Our strength comes from the collective contributions of everyone who scripts the success story of Linde every day - the esteemed Board of Directors, employees, suppliers and all stakeholders of the Company. We are grateful to our Board of Directors for providing us with valuable strategic guidance and helping us align to our mission more impactfully, our employees whose knowledge, skills and efforts form the core of the organization, our business partners for helping us in propelling our business, and our customers for their constant support and trust, which motivates to raise our bars at every step.

The world economy is gradually on its path of post-Covid recovery, however there are set-backs on account of the geopolitical impact of the ongoing Russia-Ukraine war, the effect of which is seen is steep hike in the prices of raw materials and denting production. We are also facing challenges against dollar crisis and inflation that is hindering the smooth flow of business. Linde Bangladesh is navigating through these challenging alleys of time. But as always, "we are confident that we will not only overcome, but learn from the current challenges and continue "Building on Strengths".

## Corporate History.

Linde plc is a leading industrial gases and engineering company in the world. In October 2018, formed by the merger of Praxair, Inc. and Linde AG, two world-class companies with nearly 140 years of shared history and successful achievements. Today we now operate as One Linde which represents the collective strengths that unite 80,000 employees more than 100 countries to leverage our extensive footprint and capabilities to fully capture the value of this merger.

In the 2022 financial year, it achieved sales of $33 billion ($30.8 billion in 2021).

## Our bequest in Bangladesh

Linde Bangladesh Limited, a member of Linde plc, has been contributing towards the development of the nation as a silent partner.  A strong in-built culture with solid work values developed and strengthened Linde Bangladesh over the years which are reflected in the performance of its employees for more than 60 years with continuous expansion in operations and business.

We sell our products to more than 35000 customers from a wide spectrum of industries running from chemicals and petrochemicals to steel. Our team of around 300 trained, motivated and professional members manage 24 hour operations at three major locations across the country to support our customers.

In Linde Bangladesh Limited we are committed to the quality of our products and services. Our motto is to ensure optimum conditions in health, safety and the environment for employees, customers and stakeholders.

## Our milestones at a glance

- 1953 Chittagong Oxygen plant was commissioned.
- 1973 Bangladesh Oxygen Limited (BOL) Incorporated in the joint stock companies and received government approval as the first fullfledged 'Company' of the newly formed country.
- 1976 First CO2 plant was introduced.
- 1979 Welding Training Centre started its journey.
- 1995 The Company changed its name from 'Bangladesh Oxygen Limited' to 'BOC Bangladesh Limited.'
- 1995 The Company has commissioned a 30TPD ASU Plant and 1st Welding Production line at Rupganj.
- 1998 2nd Welding production line at Rupganj was commissioned.
- 1999 20 TPD Shitalpur plant was acquired.
- 2000 ASPEN at shitalpur and LPG Bottling plant at Bogra commissioned.
- 2004 Moved into newly built Corporate Office.
- 2006 The BOC Bangladesh Limited acquired by the Linde Group, Germany.
- 2010 Achieved BDT 100 Crore EBITDA.
- 2011   3rd Welding production line at Rupganj was commissioned.
- 2011 The Company changed its name from 'BOC Bangladesh Limited' to 'Linde Bangladesh Limited.'
- 2012 4th Welding production line at Rupganj was commissioned.
- 2013 LPG Plant at Bogra decommissioned.
- 2017 Rupganj 100 TPD ASU plant was commissioned.
- 2019 36 TPD CO2 plant was commissioned at Rupganj

## Linde at the forefront during COVID crisis.

## During the Covid epidemic, we have shouldered new responsibilities, to work and create impact in a manner that matters for our people, our clients, our community, and our planet.

Linde plc is a leading global industrial gases and engineering company with 80,000 employees and 140 years of shared history, operating in more than 100 countries. Linde Bangladesh Limited, a member of Linde plc, has been contributing towards the development of the nation as a silent partner for more than 50 years.

The COVID-19 outbreak, spread rapidly and broadly around the globe. The pandemic had a deep impact on the well-being of many nations, with major implications on public health, society, safety, and the economy. As an organization, we stood through this predicament with solidarity and proficiency, backed by our solid work ethics, innovation, strong commitment towards our customers and patients, and competence of our employees.

Despite lingering uncertainties created by COVID, we have acted responsibly to provide excellent support to the nation. Our teams' ability to step up on daily basis, at every stage of the crisis, helped us to make the difference by maintaining impressive services to our valued stakeholders. The key contributors behind Linde Bangladesh Limited's incredible support during this challenging period were undoubtedly our passionate and dedicated employees who kept our plants steadily operational despite all difficulties.

The pandemic also exposed us, as an organization, to new set of responsibilities. To take care of our patients, we have installed equipment for emergency and temporary care facilities and increased the supply of medical oxygen to help our people. With multiple teams working remotely, bringing forth the challenges of communication, and above all financial uncertainty creating new anxieties for the organizations and employees, taking steps to strengthen employee engagement and morale is critical for the company. When the health crisis was at its worst and the healthcare facility was overwhelmed, we increased the amount of oxygen produced at our air separation plants and worked very closely with local healthcare providers to anticipate, monitor, and supply according to the demand.

During the pandemic, Linde Bangladesh Limited provided an extensive support to the government as well as private institutions by supplying various medical products and services. The company has also attempted to spread awareness to the nation through newspapers about preventive measures to diminish the spread of the virus.

Our healthcare business became a second line of defense in the fight against the virus and we thank our people for their dedication and determination during these unprecedented times. We have, unfortunately, lost many of our colleagues around the world, to the dooms of the pandemic. Our thoughts and prayers go out for their families. To contribute to the underserved communities, who were especially impacted by this pandemic, Linde's Global Giving program extended its help with over $10 million contribution to various social outreach programs.

To address the skyrocketing demand, we imported oxygen from our neighboring country India. In this effort, the company received extraordinary support and help from all governmental authorities in Bangladesh as well as from Linde India Limited. We are thankful to all who helped us in handling the crisis.

Linde Bangladesh Limited continued to extend its services to the community through social outreach activities. In line with the CSR activities of the previous years, your Company donated an oxygen booth to Khulna Medical College Hospital (KMCH), one of the largest COVID-19 facilities in Khulna, to provide support for emergency patients amid the COVID-19 surge in the country. The Oxygen Booth was managed by the hospital authorities and was set up near the emergency wing entrance of the hospital, covering a 120 sq ft area.

Currently, one of Linde's key priorities is to ensure continuous supply of oxygen and essential medical gases to support hospitals, doctors, nurses and other medical professionals. Our efficient teams are working continuously to install new liquid oxygen tanks and to augment cylinder filling plants. The pandemic taught us valuable and transformational lessons. We stride forward to our future by converting our learnings into actions which would help us making our world more productive.

## Notice of Extra-Ordinary General Meeting.

This notice is hereby given to all the shareholders and members of Linde Bangladesh Limited that in accordance with the direction of the Hon'ble High Court by an order dated 16.03.2023 passed in the Company Matter No. 107 of 2023, an Extra-Ordinary General Meeting of Linde Bangladesh Limited will be held virtually by using digital platform through the following link https://meetbd.live/egm2023linde, on Thursday 11 May 2023 at 10:30 am to transact the following business:

## APPROVAL OF THE SCHEME OF ARRANGEMENT FOR DEMERGER

To consider, and if thought fit, to pass the following resolutions as special resolution, with or without modification:

'RESOLVED THAT Draft Scheme of Arrangement for Demerger of Linde Bangladesh Limited ('the Scheme') be and is hereby approved and subject to sanction of the Scheme by the High Court Division of the Supreme Court of Bangladesh and subject to approval of the Scheme by the creditors of Linde Bangladesh Limited and creditors and shareholders of Linde Industries Pvt. Ltd., in accordance with the provisions of Section 228 and 229 of the Companies Act, 1994, let Demerged Undertaking of Linde Bangladesh Limited be demerged and vested to Linde Industries Pvt. Ltd. in terms of the Scheme.'

The shareholders and members of the Company are being requested to attend the meeting on the date, time and at the place mentioned above.

By order of the Board

<!-- image -->

Abu Mohammad Nisar FCS Company Secretary 12 March 2023

## Notes:

- 1 Shareholders whose names will appear in the share register of the Company or in the depository register as on Record Date i.e., 2 April 2023 will be eligible to attend and vote at the Extra-Ordinary General Meeting.
- 2 The Members will be able to submit their questions/comments and vote electronically 24 hours before commencement of the EGM and during the EGM. For logging in to the system, the Members need to put their 16-digit Beneficial Owner (BO) ID number and other credential as proof of their identity by visiting the link: https://meetbd.live/egm2023linde.
- 3 A member eligible to attend the Extra-Ordinary General Meeting is entitled to appoint a proxy to attend and vote on his/her behalf. No person shall act as proxy unless he/she is entitled to be present and vote in his/her own right. The 'Proxy Form', duly filled, signed and stamped at BDT 20 must be deposited at the office or e-mail at info.bd@linde.com no later than 72 hours before commencement of the EGM.
- 4 The Scheme of Arrangement for Demerger shall be kept open in the registered office of Linde Bangladesh Limited and Linde Industries Pvt. Ltd. 285, Tejgaon Industrial Area, Dhaka-1208, and is available for inspection by all the Concerned (during Office Hours). These also available on the website of the Company: http://www.linde.com.bd.

Registered Office Corporate Office 285 Tejgaon I/A Dhaka 1208

By order of the Board

## Board of Directors.

Moloy Banerjee Chairman since 2021

<!-- image -->

Mr. Moloy Banerjee is the Head of the newly formed 'Region ASEAN &amp; South Asia (RSE)' cluster. In addition, Mr. Banerjee is the Chairman of Linde Bangladesh Board and Director of Ceylon Oxygen Limited.

Prior to the current role, Mr. Moloy Banerjee served as the Head of South Asia, holding this position since May 2015. Mr. Banerjee has earlier served as Managing Director of Linde India Limited between July 2013 to June 2019.

Mr. Banerjee joined BOC India Limited in January 1997 as Construction Manager, Contracts Division. During his 20+ years tenure with BOC India Limited/Linde India Limited, he has held various other positions, including:

- Deputy Country Head, India
- Head of Tonnage Account Management for RSE &amp; RSP (International Assignment in Singapore during 20112012)
- Head of Marketing Sales - India
- Marketing Manager
- Project Development Manager - Global Process Systems
- General Manager - Process Systems
- Business Development Manager - HO Contracts

Mr. Banerjee started his career as Sr Construction Engineer at Linde India Limited in 1987.  Thereafter he was associated with Warren Industrial Ltd as Deputy Manager Project from 1994 to 1996.

Mr. Banerjee holds a bachelor's degree in Mechanical Engineering from the Indian Institute of Technology, Kanpur.

Bibhabasu Sengupta Managing Director since April 2023

<!-- image -->

Mr. Bibhabasu Sengupta joined the Board of Directors as an alternate Director to Mr. Sujeet Kumar Pai on 8 February 2023 and was appointed as the Managing Director of Linde Bangladesh Limited on 1 April 2023. Prior to joining Linde Bangladesh Limited, he worked as the Director - Marketing &amp; Communications for Linde South Asia Services Pvt. Ltd. (LSAS) from 2020 at Bangalore, India. During these three years, he was heading the Marketing &amp; Communications function for India, Bangladesh and Sri Lanka, as part of the Linde South Asia Leadership Team. He also had multiple overarching responsibilities for Marketing &amp; Communications for the ASEAN countries since the integration of Linde South Asia and ASEAN countries in 2022.

Mr. Sengupta started his career with Larsen &amp; Toubro Limited in 1997, and worked as an Engineer at various Petrochemical, Infrastructure and Refinery Project Sites across India till 2005.

His stint in Industrial Gases started with the Project Engineering Division (PED) of erstwhile BOC India Ltd. in 2005.

Earlier to the Linde - Praxair merger, Mr. Sengupta had worked for Praxair India Pvt. Ltd. from 2007 to 2020 in various capacities of C&amp;MES, Unit Business Owner for Packaged Gases Business, National Sales Manager for Packaged Gases in India, Head of Product Management, Marketing &amp; Communications and Head of Productivity. He won the Hallmark Award in 2021, as the Transformational Leader for 2021, and another Excellence Award for Sales Integration, as the best Marketing Project for 2021. Thereafter, in 2022, he won the Excellence Award for Profitable Growth in MPG Business.

Mr. Bibhabasu Sengupta holds a Mechanical Engineering Diploma from the Govt. of West Bengal, India, and have undertaken Executive Learning Program from IIM - Ahmedabad, India on Advanced Customer Analytics.

Rupali H Chowdhury Director since 2018

<!-- image -->

Ms. Rupali Chowdhury is an MBA from IBA, University of Dhaka, and completed her Graduation with Honours in Chemistry from the University of Chittagong.

She started her career with Multinational Pharmaceutical &amp; Chemical Company, 'Ciba Geigy (Bangladesh) Limited', in 1984 in the department of Planning Information and Control and worked there for about six and a half years.  She was the Brand Manager while leaving Ciba Geigy (Bangladesh) Limited in 1990.

Ms. Rupali Chowdhury joined Berger Paints Bangladesh Limited in 1990 as Planning Manager and during her tenure she worked for various departments such as Marketing, Sales, Distribution, Planning and Systems under different supervisory capacities.

Ms. Chowdhury was promoted to the position of Managing Director of the Company on 1 January 2008. She is also the Managing Director of Jenson &amp; Nicholson (Bangladesh) Limited, a 100% subsidiary of Berger Paints Bangladesh Limited and Director of Berger Becker Bangladesh Limited, a joint venture between Berger Paints Bangladesh Limited and Becker Industrial Coatings Holding AB Sweden and Berger Fosroc Limited (a joint venture between Berger Paints Bangladesh Limited and Fosroc International Limited, UK) which was formed during her tenure as Managing Director. Ms. Chowdhury has been appointed as an Independent Director of Bata Shoe Company (Bangladesh) Limited effective April 2018.

Ms. Chowdhury has been selected as a Commercially Important Person (CIP) for her outstanding performance in the Industrial sector.

Tanjib-Ul Alam Director since 2019

<!-- image -->

Mr. Tanjib-ul Alam, barrister-at-law, has been practicing in Bangladesh since 1997 and in the Appellate Division of the Supreme Court of Bangladesh since 2005. He completed LL.B. Hons. from the University of London in 1996 and was called to the Bar of England from the Lincoln's Inn, UK, in 1997. His area of practice includes arbitration, corporate, cross-border investment, energy, oil and gas, merger and acquisition, project finance, securities and telecommunication. In corporate sector, he has been involved in number of ground-breaking project finance and IPOs in Bangladesh. He has substantial experience in representing energy companies in innumerable arbitrations and litigations, including mergers of energy companies and acted as lead counsel in many syndicated transactions involving rental power projects, and advised many clients during establishment of gas based and coal based power projects.

As legal consultant, Mr. Alam was actively involved in drafting the Bangladesh Energy Regulatory Commission Act, Bangladesh Telecommunication Act, and Information and Communication Technology Act for Bangladesh. He was the Lead Consultant for International Finance Corporation ('IFC') in drafting new Companies Act for Bangladesh. Besides, Mr. Alam has extensive experience of consultancy for Institutional Reform International Inc. (IRIS), USA, World Bank, UNDP and other reputed international organizations on diversified areas of law. Mr. Alam has also been involved as a lawyer in number of leading constitutional law cases during the past decade which have far reaching impact in the rule of law and constitutionalism in the legal history of Bangladesh. Mr. Alam heads the renowned law firm, Tanjib Alam and Associates, based in Dhaka. His law firm, Tanjib Alam and Associates, has acquired the reputation of being one of the most talented team of professionals in Bangladesh having experience of working with world's leading international law firms. Mr. Alam has been serving as a Member of the Board of Directors of Biman Bangladesh Airlines, the national flag carrier airline.

MV Pavan Director since 2019

<!-- image -->

Mr. MV Pavan is a Director at Linde Bangladesh Limited. He is the Vice President of Praxair India Pvt Limited, Bengaluru, India and heads Finance, IT &amp; Procurement divisions.  He also has additional responsibility as Director of Praxair India.

Mr. Pavan joined Praxair India Private Limited in July 2018. Prior to 2018, he was the Finance Director of Praxair Gulf Industrial Gases, Abu Dhabi, UAE, responsible for finance function for Middle East businesses, between 2013 &amp; 2018. He was also holding various roles in finance and business development between 2004 to 2012 at Praxair India Private Limited, Bengaluru, India.  Prior to Praxair India, he worked as an Associate with KPMG Risk Advisory Services at Bengaluru, India.

Mr. Pavan is a qualified Chartered Accountant, passed out in the year 2001 from Institute of Chartered Accountants of India. He also holds a Bachelor of Commerce degree from Bangalore University.

## Chairman's Statement.

Dear Shareholders, Dear Shareholders,

<!-- image -->

It is my pleasure to welcome you to the 50th Annual General Meeting of your Company, Linde Bangladesh Limited and present to you the business results for the fiscal year 2022.

We demonstrated tremendous resilience the' before COVID impacted 2020 and 2021. As a well-knit team, we at Linde Bangladesh Limited were able to save millions of lives and extend support to hospitals and patients during the pandemic.  Despite the uncertainty caused by the outbreak, we have acted responsibly to provide excellent support to the community at large. The epidemic made us aware of our responsibility as vital service providers to the Nation, and this has not changed. Notwithstanding these headwinds, the company remained a silent partner in the nation's ongoing development.

While we were recovering from the effects of COVID-19, the geopolitical conflict in Ukraine sparked another crisis and dragged us back into a difficult situation. This had a cascading effect on our economy and our business in Bangladesh was no exception. The economy was on the back-foot in 2022 due to low foreign currency reserves, a higher US dollar rate and market crisis, lower remittances, gas and power shortage, and fewer exports. In the past six months, Bangladesh's foreign reserves have dropped below $32bn from $39bn while the value of the taka has fallen by 27 percent from 84 to the dollar to 107.

In face of these geopolitical headwinds, despite our best efforts, the company's financial results were inadequate, with lower profits than in the preceding year. Team Linde accepts collective responsibility for this performance, and we are determined to convert some of the key learnings of this period into constructive outcomes for future.

Our Hardgoods Business faced extreme volatility in input cost due to fluctuations in international raw material prices (Steel &amp; Rutile). Moreover, due to limited opportunities and weak demand, the market remained price sensitive and saw increased competition intensity from both domestic and Chinese players. To mitigate the business impact, there was a Business Turnaround Workshop that was organized in May last year wherein there were a slew of strategic initiatives undertaken to improve the business performance. However, the country faced severe headwinds in shipbuilding, infrastructure realty projects which has resulted in decreased demand for welding electrodes and resulted in lower performance as compared to previous years.

Following the Organizational restructuring completed earlier this year, the Company implemented measures to improve operational productivity. Through lean initiatives and automation, the company saw a gradual increase in output per shift. The volatility of raw material input costs remains a source of concern, and our Supply Chain Team is taking the necessary steps to import key ingredients from the most costeffective sources around the world.

The Company has terminated its association with Connect Distribution Limited (Distributor) due to breach of contract and are now reaching out to our customers directly in the served region. The Company has opened its own Warehouse cum Depot at Khulna and Rangpur in November and December 2022 respectively. The response from end users has been encouraging.

The drop in Medical Oxygen consumption post COVID in Q1 - 2022 also resulted in a drop in revenues for the healthcare segment as compared to the preceding year.

Due to the reasons mentioned above, our performance witnessed a downtrend as compared to 2021. The financial performance of the Company, in brief:

- Profit before taxation declined 24.04 percent to BDT 1,135 million
- Income decreased 27.94 percent to BDT 883 million
- Earnings per share declined 27.94 percent to BDT 58.04

We believe the Company has dedicated, efficient and motivated workforce and we will strive to achieve our business results in FY 2023. Linde will continue to create positive impact by developing employees' ability to engage and go that extra mile for our customers, patients, and society at large. Linde Bangladesh Limited is committed to improving the communities where we live and work. I have confidence in our ability to outperform given our resilient business style and productivity opportunities to mitigate any macroeconomic headwinds.

Compared to the previous year, in 2022 overall revenue of your Company declined by 4.13%, profit before taxation declined by 24.04% and earnings declined by 27.94%. Bulk business increased by 22%. Hardgoods sales considerably declined by about 2.4%, for reasons mentioned earlier. Packaged Gases &amp; products have grown for 7% over prior year. Post pandemic, healthcare business was struggling to retain the volume where market was having surplus molecules and loosing pressure on products versus demand. Details of the segment wise business performance and financial results are included in the Report

of the Board of Directors and in the Auditors' Report and Financial Statements.

The Directors of your Company have recommended a dividend of 42.00 per share or 420 % for the year ended 31 December 2022. This would require a payout of 639.17 million BDT.

The Linde Bangladesh Limited Deliver team's outstanding safety record continues. There was no major event throughout the year. Besides, they achieved 20% productivity out of their previous year cost stack.

During 2022, Linde Bangladesh Limited secured various awards and recognitions, including Gold Medal in the Oil, Gas &amp; Energy Category at the Best Corporate Award 2021 from the Institute of Cost and Management Accountants of Bangladesh (ICMAB) based on its performance in 2021. We had received the same Award from ICMAB in 2021.

Safety is one of the core values of Linde that influences our decisions, actions and behaviours. Safety is integral part of the activities or business we do at Linde. Indeed, our highest priority, which is Safety. 2022 started with 10 resolutions of safety &amp; the slogan was 'Zero Today. Make it Happen'. Our goal is to ensure no harm to people, environment, or the communities where we operate. Aligned with the HSE policy, our target is zero incidents.

In 2022, our safety performance was excellent in all the areas of operational, transport &amp; process safety. There was no Major Event (ME), no Fatality Potential Event (FPE), no major transport safety event (zero Level-1 &amp; zero level-2 event) &amp; no recordable workplace injury both for either employees and or contractors.

The company completed 100% of the leading indicators of safety for 2022; such as audit &amp; assessment, Major Hazard Review Program (MHRP) certificate, zero overdue Corrective Actions Request (CAR), driver risk profile management, virtual &amp; on-site training for employees &amp; contractors, incident management, etc. Also 94% achievement of annual operating plan (AOP) related to HSE &amp; Quality, periodic review call by site sponsor &amp; managements, meeting involving all stakeholders.

To improve behavioral safety of the workforce 'Safeguard Program' was launched at Rupganj site. This year, Linde Bangladesh celebrated the 'Global Quality Week 2022' and "Global Safety Commitment Week" in all locations including operation sites and sales centers to grow more awareness. We also received Food Safety Certificate for Production &amp; Storage of Liquid Nitrogen and Carbon Di-Oxide.

Our human capital is represented by the skills, diversity, passion, enthusiasm, ideas, youthfulness, and experience of our employees. We invest in the development and well-being of our people. We seek to leverage that cultural diversity in support of our vision to be the best performing global industrial gases and engineering company. Actively fostering an inclusive work environment that values diversity in its many forms has a significant, positive impact on a Company's performance. Diverse experiences and viewpoints strengthen teams and can even result in a better understanding of customer needs.

We ensure that the award and recognition system is performance-based and competitive.  To inspire workers, employee-friendly policies have been implemented. We have introduced proper usage of digital robust global &amp; local systems, process and tools and continuously improve them in line with business requirements.

To become more efficient in 2023 we have restructured the organization to have a competitive edge in the market. Excellent Industrial Relations was maintained throughout the year. Employee survey was carried out to measure the engagement and satisfaction index and accordingly action plan was taken. Human resources development strategy is anchored on equipping our people with a diverse set of skills that enables innovation and creativity and will allow them to thrive today and into the future. Digital platforms are used so the employee get to experience various learning opportunity.

In 2022, Linde Bangladesh Limited was focused to drive Information Technology (IT) security related initiatives by implementing a series of security enhancements based on the Zero Trust principle 'trust nothing, always verify'. Zero trust represents the future of cybersecurity. As part of these Linde Bangladesh Limited employees are getting increased multi factor authentication while working from home or anywhere outside office network through Global Protect Remote Access. Existing WAN architecture has been upgraded to Software defined Wide Area Network (SDWAN) which is ensuring more reliable, cost-effective, and secure network connectivity.

As a part of the Digitalization thrust, Linde Bangladesh Limited implemented barcode-based inventory system for Hardgoods spares which makes the inventory process smooth and automated. The SMS solution for Hardgoods customer was also rolled out in 2022, so that customers get an automated SMS with price and delivery date when a sales order is created.

We believe strongly that part of our commitment to making the world more productive is making our communities more successful, more equitable and more resilient. We support, maintain and strengthen projects focused in the areas of the environment, diversity and education, community support and health.

As a part of our CSR outreach, we partnered with an NGO in providing livelihood through Tool kits and Skill development to almost 200 plus youths from Teknaf &amp; Ukhiya Upazilla in August 2022. This will help

create more job opportunity and sustainable employment generation. Later in September, Company extended Entrepreneurship support for Hitampur fisherfolk women by providing them with Fishing Net &amp; equipment. Women in the profession of fishing is new in Bangladesh and as part of our vision we actively encourage and support diversity in all professional arena.

For more than 50 years, Linde Bangladesh Limited has operated with flawlessly effective company governance. 'Building on Strengths' more than 50 years of company longevity and success serve as the cornerstone of our firm foundation. Transparency and ethics are other aspects of good company governance. It strengthens accountability, highest integrity, and openness within the Company's current systems, assists us in upholding our legal and regulatory obligations, and ensures that results are distributed in an open and honest manner. In every way consistent with our code of business integrity.

We always work to accomplish our objectives in an ethical manner, and we value diversity and inclusion in order to draw in, nurture, and keep the best talent and create high-performing teams. As part of the Board of Directors of the Company's primary duty, we anticipate management, workers, and business partners to conduct themselves in a manner that complies with our Code of Business Integrity.

The success of Linde has always been based on the Board of Directors' capacity to work closely together, effectively serve the interests of shareholders, and take a firm stand on regulations.

## Dear Shareholders

While we were recovering from the effects of COVID-19, the geopolitical conflict in Ukraine triggered another crisis and dragged us back into a difficult situation. This had a cascading effect on our economy and our business in Bangladesh was no exception. Your company's business success has been negatively impacted in 2022.

In comparing the performance of your Company in FY 2022, I might have chosen an image that was not very exciting. Though, Bulk business has significantly increased over prior year followed by retention of key accounts and expansion with prospect pipelines. Packaged Gases &amp; products also have shown a positive progress over previous year. Your Company continues today to be one of the most profitable companies in the country. It is also among the very top companies in the country in terms of dividend payout. However, we are not completely unfamiliar with situation like the present.

In the past we saw, your company has excelled admirably, increasing Revenues and Earnings and allowing your Board to recommend high dividends.

Linde Bangladesh Limited's teams across the country have been working relentlessly for over 50 years to support our customers and patients silently, while being committed to the any challenges alongside governments and healthcare workers. Today, Linde Bangladesh Limited is competent and consistent organization, with new and well-functioning production facilities and a comprehensive distribution system. I firmly believe that we can continue to forge this future, construct it, and make our world a more productive place to exist in it by working together.

Although our situation was not favorable for FY22 but moving forward we are optimistic that Linde Bangladesh Limited's talented, devoted, and motivated employees will take the initiative in FY23. As you are aware, a growing market has made business for your company lucrative but competitive. In the hardgoods market, there are already a lot of rivals, and during this crisis, more are likely to join. We continue to be the leading player in the Gases Business strongly driven by organic growth and application driven opportunities.

I have the honor and privilege of expressing my sincere appreciation to the Board Members and shareholders for their unwavering belief in us. I thank the employees of the Company for their hard and dedicated work and exhort them to greater effort to meet up the challenges of the times ahead. We owe our gratitude to our customers, shareholders, suppliers, bankers, government officials, and agencies.

Take care. Stay well and safe.

Thank you, ladies, and gentlemen.

Moloy Banerjee Dhaka, 12 March 2023

## Report of the Board of Directors.

The Board of Directors of Linde Bangladesh Limited is pleased to present the Directors' Report and Auditors' Report together with the Financial Statements of the Company for the year ended 31 December 2022. The Directors' report highlights the key business activities that drove the performance of the Company and presents the necessary disclosures in relation to good corporate governance.

## Industry Outlook and Possible Future Developments

This year, the world witnessed the continuing pandemic, the war in Ukraine and high inflation-circumstances that pushed many companies to scale back their 2022 growth forecasts. The aftermath of the COVID19 pandemic which were made worse by the Russia-Ukraine conflict, has disrupted the entire world and plunged us into a great crisis in every sector of the economy like power, fuel, food, supply chain, foreign exchange, among others. Inflation is at a 40-year high, supply chains are stretched, and the war in Ukraine is ongoing.

Both the International Monetary Fund and the World Bank have painted a gloomy forecast for the global economy for 2023 as uncertainty persists amidst the protracting Russia-Ukraine war, the volatility in the international energy market and high inflation. The impending threat of recession looms large.

In these difficult times, the increase in prices of all necessary commodities has put our government under huge pressure to control inflation. Continuously increasing price in power, gas, commodity and inflation rate is making common man's life more difficult. Like other industrial sectors of our country, the steel industry also faced numerous challenges causing a substantial increase in our main raw material price at mid-year. The weakening of Taka against the US Dollar added further to our raw material import costs.

In 2022, the Linde Bangladesh Limited delivery crew continued its outstanding safety performance. We continued excellent service to our valued customers and maintained a high level of safety to remain the market leader in medical and industrial gases business. Distribution team delivered an impressive 20% productivity improvement through various initiatives. Performance of Bulk Industrial Gases improved versus previous year.  The sales team focused on retention of important accounts and developing the prospect pipeline for addition of new accounts. Liquid CO2 registered double digit growth for successive two years. Due to the slowdown in economic activity and the high price of steel, which deterred new investments, the market for hardgoods was negatively impacted. Sales of the hardgoods business, light fabrication segment were also affected by the real estate market's slowdown and the weak activity in bus body manufacturing. Despite the continuing escalation of competition in the light fabrication market, Linde Bangladesh Limited expanded its presence in this segment.

The healthcare business retained all its liquid medical oxygen customers. Pricing initiatives to pass on the escalation of input costs were successfully implemented. The healthcare business also holds a healthy pipeline of growth prospects which will strengthen the business going forward.

The committed team at Linde Bangladesh Limited carried out major projects, increased our profitability, kept our plants operating in the face of extreme adversity, and provided our valued customers with necessary products. Despite the challenges, the team managed to deliver a credible financial performance .

## Business performance

The Company's revenue and earnings in 2022 were lower than in the previous year, due to the challenges highlighted above. The revenue of the Company for the year 2022 was Tk. 4,873 m as against Tk. 5,083 m of 2021.  The revenue is derived from following segments:

| ## Segments  2022  2021  MBDT  MBDT  Bulk Gases  816  668  Packaged Gas and Products (PG&amp;P)  3,010  3,048  Healthcare  1,047  1,367  4,873  5,083   |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

Bulk gases comprise of liquid industrial oxygen, liquid nitrogen, liquid argon, and liquid carbon dioxide. PG&amp;P business is comprised of mild steel electrodes and compressed industrial gases. Healthcare business is comprised of medical gases, medical equipment, and medical pipeline.

Details of business performance for business segment i.e., Bulk, PG &amp; P (Packaged Gas and Products) and Healthcare are provided in the subsequent sections.

## Bulk

Bulk business has increased by 22% over the previous year, supported by consolidation of the base business and leveraging a strong prospect pipeline to secure new wins. A significant number of pricing initiatives were undertaken by the business recover cost increase. Despite the availability of surplus molecules and fierce price competition in the market, bulk business has been able retain all its customers and achieved the stated growth. Volume of all the product lines i.e., Liquid Oxygen, Liquid Nitrogen, Liquid Argon, Liquid CO2 has recovered sharply with double digit growth from last year with CO2 registering 33% growth. The existing UNION plant was fully loaded last year, and the additional demand had to be supplemented through import. Market segments that contributed majorly were Livestock, Food &amp; Beverages for LIN, Bicycle export for LAR, Food &amp; Beverages for LCO2. Apart from this, the business strengthened the prospect pipeline of new accounts which will positively contribute to the business in 2023.

## PG&amp;P (packaged gas &amp; products)

PG&amp;P revenue increased 7% from the previous year. The business also adopted pricing measures to offset rise in fuel and other utility costs. The business's profitability was maintained by timely pricing decisions based on costs. Specialty gases also contributed to growth through Helium and other special gases like SF6.

In 2022, hardgoods business saw a 2.4% decline from the prior year. The Light Fabrication (LF) segment volume was down 5% and had the biggest impact. Real estate and other fabrication activities slowed down as a result of the unstable steel price in the global market, which impacted sales in the light fabrication segment.

Sales in the shipbuilding sector was also impacted in 2022 as a result of the high price of steel, which discouraged new investment and reduced demand for ships in the local market. The sales volume in the shipbuilding sector fell by 13%. Despite the entry of multiple new competitors, Linde Bangladesh Limited continues to retain a major market position in the Electrode business.

## Healthcare

Post COVID-19, the healthcare business saw a slump in volume due to low demand and excess supply. Despite the significant demand shortfall as compared to previous year, the healthcare segment retained all liquid medical oxygen customers and implemented pricing initiatives particularly in the private hospitals. In the compressed medical market, we have started marketing our new products like Portable Cryogenic Containers, Entonox, etc. which is going to be launched by Q1 of 2023. Apart from this, there are prospective customers in the pipeline which will positively impact the company's performance in 2023.

## Financial Results

Due to slow post covid recovery and macroeconomic headwinds, 2022 was a challenging year for Linde Bangladesh Limited. Disciplined price management and fixed cost containment initiatives enabled the Company to limit the impact. Sales and profit declined by 4.13% and 27.94% respectively in 2022 compared to the previous year. Gross profit declined by 13.59% over previous year.

## Risks &amp; Concerns

The Company has a defined business risk assessment process which has been illustrated under Corporate Governance Chapter and the notes to the Financial Statements.

## Minority Shareholders

The Board of Directors of Linde Bangladesh Limited believes that through transparent business practices and appropriate disclosure of the Company's material information, the rights and interests of the minority shareholders have been properly safeguarded. The timely holding of the annual general meeting, the adoption of the annual financial statements, the election of new directors, the appointment of statutory and compliance auditors, dividend payments, and other issues requiring the involvement and approval of shareholders. To protect the rights of its minority shareholders and the interests of all stakeholders, the Board of Directors is dedicated to upholding the highest standards of governance that foster honesty, openness, and accountability.

## Related Party Transactions

Transactions with related parties are made on arm's length basis and are in the ordinary course of business. Detailed transactions with related parties are described at notes 31 of the Financial Statements of this Annual Report.

## Internal Control

The Company maintains a sound internal control system that gives reasonable assurance that the Company's resources are safeguarded, and the financial position of the Company is well managed. The internal control framework is regularly reviewed by the Audit Committee in each meeting and reported to the Board of Directors. Group Internal Audit team conducts audit to evaluate appropriateness of internal control. The findings and subsequent follow up with remedial status are reported to the Audit Committee and are escalated to the Group Internal Audit. This report further encloses a detailed discussion on the Corporate Governance.

## Future Plan

Linde always intends to maintain long-standing relationship with our valued customers by providing more value-added, world-class services such as critical industrial gases for manufacturing, life-saving medical products for healthcare and hardgoods products. The business's outlook for 2023 will be favourably impacted by the healthcare industry's pipeline of accounts for the upcoming year.

Linde Bangladesh Limited has been working across the nation for more than 50 years and harder than ever to assist our clients and patients. We plan to invest in more advanced technology in gases and other business to enhance our production process in near future.

Through upgrading our existing production and research and development facilities, we believe that we would be able to keep up with of industry trends and meet the growing demands of our customers. However, initiatives are being taken to expand other existing business segments soon.

## Dividends

The Directors recommended a final dividend of Taka 42.00 per share for the year, involving a payout of Taka 639.17 m subject to approval at the Annual General Meeting. The dividend percentage for the year would be 420 percent and total dividend payout for the year would amount to Taka 639.17 m (Taka 837.01 m in 2021).

## Transfer the Unclaimed Dividends to Capital Market Stabilization Fund (CMSF)

Ensuring all applicable legal requirements of Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission, the Company has deposited the amount of BDT. 84,867,810.90 to CMSF since inception 1985 to 2017 and 2018 respectively, which was held in Company for 3 (three) or more years against unclaimed or undistributed or unsettled cash dividends.

## Unclaimed Dividend as on 31 December 2022

Due to the necessity of complying with the law by the Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission (Capital Market Stabilization Fund) Rules, 2021, we transferred the funds to the CMSF on behalf of Linde Bangladesh Limited, who have not claimed these dividends.

The unclaimed/unpaid dividend holders list is placed on the website for the benefit of shareholders of the company. Shareholders who have not cashed their dividends relating to the dividends are requested to contact the Secretarial Department of the company for issue of unclaimed or unpaid dividends.

## Additional statements on regulatory disclosure

The Directors of the Company have included the other following disclosures:

- I. The financial statements prepared by the management of the Company present fairly its state of affairs, the result of its operations, cash flows and changes in equity;
- II. Proper books of accounts of the Company have been maintained;
- III. Appropriate accounting policies have been consistently applied in preparation of the financial statements and that the accounting estimates are based on reasonable and prudent judgment;
- IV. All deviations from the last year's operating results of the Company have been highlighted under the above Financial Results;
- V. The summarized key operating and financial data of at least preceding five years (2017-2022) is set out in page # 24.
- VI. All transactions with related parties have been made on a commercial basis and the basis was the principle of 'Arm's Length Transactions'. Related party transactions have been presented in notes to the Financial Statements;
- VII. There is no Extra-Ordinary gain or loss during the year;
- VIII.  An explanation on any significant variance that occurs between Quarterly Financial performances and Annual Financial Statements;
- IX. During the year, the Company has paid a total of Tk 420,000 as Board meeting attendance fees. The remuneration of Directors has been mentioned in the Notes to the Financial Statements;

## The Directors also report that:

a) The Managing Director and Chief Financial Officer have certifi¬ed to the Board that they have reviewed the financial statements for the year and to the best of their knowledge believe that these statements do not contain any materially untrue statements or omit any material fact or contain statements that might be misleading.

b) The Managing Director and Chief Financial Officer have certifi¬ed to the Board that they have reviewed the financial statements for the year and to the best of their knowledge believe that these statements together present a true and fair view of the Company's affairs and are in compliance with existing accounting standards and applicable laws;

c) The Managing Director and Chief Financial Officer have further certifi¬ed to the Board that there are to the best of their knowledge and belief, no transactions entered into by the Company during the year which are fraudulent, illegal or violation of the Company's Code of Conduct.

The Board of Directors Linde Bangladesh Limited Corporate Office 285 Tejgaon Industrial Area Dhaka-1208, Bangladesh

Subject: Declaration on Financial Statements for the year ended on 31 December 2022

Dear Sirs,

Pursuant to the condition No. 1(5)(xxvi) imposed vide the Commission's Notification No. BSEC/CMRRCD/2006-158/207/Admin/80 Dated 3 June 2018 under section 2CC of the Securities and Exchange Ordinance, 1969, we do hereby declare that:

1.  The Financial Statements of Linde Bangladesh Limited for the year ended on 31 December 2022 have been prepared in compliance with International Accounting Standards (IAS) or International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS), as applicable in the Bangladesh and any departure there from has been adequately disclosed;
2.  The estimates and judgments related to the financial statements were made on a prudent and reasonable basis, in order for the financial statements to reveal a true and fair view;
3.  The form and substance of transactions and the Company's state of affairs have been reasonably and fairly presented in its financial statements;
4.  To ensure above, the Company has taken proper and adequate care in installing a system of internal control and maintenance of accounting records;
5.  Our internal auditors have conducted periodic audits to provide reasonable assurance that the established policies and procedures of the Company were consistently followed; and
6.  The management's use of the going concern basis of accounting in preparing the financial statements is appropriate and there exists no material uncertainty related to events or conditions that may cast significant doubt on the Company's ability to continue as a going concern.

In this regard, we also certify that:

- i. We have reviewed the financial statements for the year ended on 31 December 2022 and that to the best of our knowledge and belief:
2. (a) These statements do not contain any materially untrue statement or omit any material fact or contain statements that might be misleading;
3. (b) These statements collectively present true and fair view of the Company's affairs and are in compliance with existing accounting standards and applicable laws.
- ii.  There are, to the best of knowledge and belief, no transactions entered into by the Company during the year which are fraudulent, illegal or in violation of the code of conduct for the Company's Board of Directors or its members.

Sincerely yours,

Report to the Shareholders of Linde Bangladesh Limited (hereinafter referred to as "the Company") on compliance on the Corporate Governance Code

We have examined the compliance status to the Corporate Governance Code by Linde Bangladesh Limited for the year ended on 31 December 2022. This Code relates to the Notification No BSEC/CMRRCD/2006-158/207/Admin/80 Dated 3 June 2018 of the Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission.

Such compliance with the Corporate Governance Code is the responsibility of the Company. Our examination was limited to the procedures and implementation thereof as adopted by the Management in ensuring compliance to the conditions of the Corporate Governance Code.

This is a scrutiny and verification and an independent audit on compliance of the conditions of the Corporate Governance Code as well as the provisions of relevant Bangladesh Secretarial Standards (BSS) as adopted by Institute of Chartered Secretaries of Bangladesh (ICSB) in so far as those standards are not inconsistent with any condition of this Corporate Governance Code.

We state that we have obtained all the information and explanations, which we have required, and after due scrutiny and verification thereof, we report that, in our opinion:

- (a) The Company has complied with the conditions of the Corporate Governance Code as stipulated in the above mentioned Corporate Governance Code issued by the Commission;
- (b) The Company has complied with the provisions of the relevant Bangladesh Secretarial Standards (BSS) as adopted by the Institute of Chartered Secretaries of Bangladesh (ICSB) as required by this Code;
- (c) Proper books and records have been kept by the Company as required under the Companies Act, 1994, the securities laws and other relevant laws; and
- (d) The governance of the Company is highly satisfactory.

Dhaka, Bangladesh

## Management's Discussion and Analysis.

## Overview

Linde Bangladesh Limited is a leading multinational company in the industrial gases business and has been a part and parcel of all the industrial and economic activities of the country for more than 50 years. Exceeding a turnover of BDT 5.8 billion, the company has been present in Bangladesh with continuous expansion in operations and business.

## (a) Accounting policies and estimation for preparation of financial statements

The financial records have been compiled in accordance with the (International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS), the Companies Act, 1994, the Securities and Exchange Rules, 2020, as well as additional rules and regulations in Bangladesh. According to the Securities and Exchange Rules, 2020, cash flows from operating activities are produced using the direct method.

The financial statements have been prepared on a historical cost basis, except for debt and equity financial assets and contingent consideration that have been measured at fair value. The financial statements provide comparative information in respect of the previous period.

International Accounting Standards (IAS) International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS), as applicable in Bangladesh, have been followed in preparation of the financial statements.

See accounting policies in note 3 of financial statements and the estimates on the director's report for more information.

## (b) Changes in accounting policies and estimation

There were no major changes in the in the accounting policies and estimates. The company has followed the same accounting policies and estimates used in the previous year and there were no material changes observed. Moreover, appropriate accounting policies have been consistently applied in preparation of the financial statements. Please see note 3 of the financial statements for more information.

## (c) Comparative analysis of financial performance, operational performance

Sales and profit declined by 4% and 28% respectively in 2022 compared to the previous year. Gross profit declined by 14% over previous year. Apart from these, Bulk business has increased by 22% over the previous year. Distribution team delivered an impressive 20% productivity improvement through various initiatives.

## (d) Comparison of financial performance with the peer industry scenario

Among the listed companies of the stock exchanges, the nature of business of Linde Bangladesh Limited is different from others. Having a distinctive operational and business models, the financial position and cash flow of the company, if compared will not be meaningful.

## (e) Financial and economic scenario of the country and the global economy

## Global Economy

The global economy is currently facing significant challenges such as a cost-of-living crisis, tight financial conditions, Russia's invasion of Ukraine, and the ongoing COVID-19 pandemic, resulting in a sharperthan-expected slowdown in economic activity and a rise in inflation. The forecast for global growth is expected to decline in the next few years, with inflation predicted to increase sharply in the short term. To tackle these issues, monetary policy should continue to aim for price stability, while fiscal policy should focus on reducing cost-of-living pressures and maintaining a tight stance aligned with monetary policy.

The European Union has implemented sanctions, including embargoes on coal imports and a ban on seaborne oil imports, which has led to a reduction in exports from Russia and a decrease in the flow of Russian pipeline gas to Europe. As a result, natural gas prices have risen steeply, causing economic repercussions such as higher energy prices, weaker consumer confidence, and slower momentum in manufacturing. The conflict has also disrupted food supply chains and led to global trade instability, resulting in rising petroleum and commodity prices, which are contributing to inflationary pressures on economies. Despite the global economy turning around after the COVID-19 pandemic, the conflict between Russia and Ukraine, along with inflation and tightening financial conditions, continues to pose significant challenges to the outlook. Policymakers are working to normalize monetary and fiscal policies that were implemented during the pandemic to help lower inflation back to target.

## Economy of Bangladesh

The Ukraine- Russia war have had a deep impact on Bangladesh economy. It has affected global oil and gas prices, as well as wheat, corn, sunflower, and various precious metals. Bangladesh imports wheat, sunflower oil, maize, and other essential commodities from Russia and Ukraine, and the war has made it difficult to import these items, leading to high import payments and a negative impact on the country's economy. The cost of imports has increased, resulting in higher retail and wholesale prices of goods in the country. The government has hiked the retail price of electricity by 5 percent bypassing the public hearing of the Bangladesh Energy Regulatory Commission. The hike will be effective from January, according to a notice from the ministry of power, energy and mineral resources.

The global impact of the Russia-Ukraine war is being felt in Bangladesh economy. The war has created supply disruptions and financial sanctions that pose serious economic challenges, with major countries including the US, the UK, Japan, and the EU suspending economic ties with Russia. Sanctions have been enforced on Russian financial institutions to disrupt transactions with the country, and small and poor countries are finding it difficult to meet their energy requirements due to high oil prices. The consequences of these challenges are seen in higher commodity and oil prices, and food prices have skyrocketed. Depending on the duration of the war, its impact at the country level will depend on the economic links with Russia and Ukraine and their exposure to the global economy.

The impact of the war is expected to intensify if it continues for a longer period, and Bangladesh will need to source commodities from the global market at competitive prices and distribute essential commodities at reduced prices through open market sales. The economic recovery will depend on the extent of public expenditure, and waste of public resources and unnecessary public expenditure should be curtailed.

## (f) Risks and concerns issues related to the financial statements

Linde Bangladesh Limited has risk management systems in place. These are continually being updated and adapted by the Company under guidance of Linde Group. The effectiveness of these systems is reviewed by group internal auditor and statutory auditor and the Board of Directors. The management of the Company made risk assessment on business and made risk mitigation plan to eliminate risks. The audit committee supports the Board in monitoring activities of management and deals with risk management issues. The audit procedures include a review of the system for early identification of risks. The Audit Committee Updates Board on annual and periodical financial statement in detail prior to its publications.

## (g) Future plan or projection or forecast for Company's operation, performance and financial position

Linde Bangladesh Limited is committed to maintaining strong and long-lasting relationships with our valued customers by continuously enhancing our services and providing world-class solutions. Our diverse range of offerings includes critical industrial gases for manufacturing, life-saving medical products for healthcare, and hardgoods products, all of which are designed to add value and improve the lives of our customers.

Looking ahead to 2023, we anticipate a favorable impact on our business from the growing pipeline of accounts in the healthcare industry. As a company, we are committed to working tirelessly to assist our clients and patients, and we have been doing so for more than 50 years in Bangladesh.

To remain at the forefront of the industry and ensure that we continue to meet the evolving needs of our customers, we are planning to

invest in more advanced technologies in gases and other areas of our business soon. By upgrading our existing production and research and development facilities, we aim to stay ahead of industry trends and meet the growing demands of our customers.

In addition to these initiatives, we are also exploring opportunities to expand our existing business segments the foreseeable future. Our ultimate goal is to provide our customers with the best possible solutions, and we are committed to achieving this by maintaining a customer-centric approach and continuously improving our servicesMaking our world more productive.

## (h) Related party transactions

The company conducts transactions with related parties in a manner consistent with arm's length standards and in the normal course of business. Any transactions with related parties are fully disclosed in the company's Annual Report, specifically in note 31 of the Financial Statements. This note provides a detailed breakdown of all transactions made with related parties, including the nature of the transactions, the amounts involved, and any terms and conditions. The company takes great care to ensure that all transactions with related parties are conducted fairly and transparently, and that they do not present any conflicts of interest. By providing this information to shareholders and other stakeholders, the company aims to maintain accountability and transparency in its dealings with related parties, and to promote trust and confidence in its operations.
